Pulley me first tension T1 and T2 nikal lo. Uske baad unhe use karke pulley ka radial and tangential force nikal lo. Tangential hoga T1 - T2 and radial hoga T1 and T2 ka vector sum and unke beech ka wrap angle hoga, par is case me radial force T1 + T2 hoga. Fir dono vertical and horizontal loading ke cases banao and un cases me max bending moment nikal lo. Un dono max bending moment ka vector sum lelo and fir bending moment aajayega. 09/11/25, 23:01 - Aditya Gupta NITT: Alright—here’s a worked solution for Q4 based on what’s readable from your scan. I’ll state every assumption I had to make from the blurry bits and then carry the design through. If any number in your paper differs, you can swap it in the formulas I’ve shown.

Given / interpreted from the sheet
	•	Power from motor to the shaft: P=15\ \text{kW}
	•	Motor pulley N_m=1250\ \text{rpm} → pulley on the shaft (B) at N=1000\ \text{rpm}
	•	Spur gear (A) on the same shaft, transmits the 15 kW to the load
	•	Pressure angle 20^\circ, module m=5\ \text{mm} → pinion (on the shaft) has z_1=20 ⇒ d_g=mz_1=100\ \text{mm}; face width b_g=12m=60\ \text{mm}
	•	Shaft material (and keys): S_{yt}\approx 400\ \text{MPa} (tough steel); use ASME: K_b=1.5,\ K_t=2.0
	•	Two deep-groove ball bearings B1, B2; “load factor” 2.5 (taken as service factor on bearing equivalent load)
	•	Belt: open belt, \mu=0.3; centre distance motor↔shaft =2.4\ \text{m}; Dunlop high-speed data: capacity =0.0118\ \text{kW per mm per ply at }v=5\ \text{m/s}. Load correction =1.2.
	•	Figure 4 distances (readable dimensions): span between bearings L=300\ \text{mm}. Feature centres ≈ 120\ \text{mm} in from each bearing (so gear at x=120\ \text{mm}, pulley at x=180\ \text{mm}). Pulley diameter marking shows \phi 250\ \text{mm}; I’ve used D_p=250\ \text{mm}.
	•	Motor below the shaft ⇒ belt pull on the shaft is vertical.

If any of those two or three geometric numbers in your original are different, plug them in the same steps.

⸻

1) Shaft torque

T=\frac{9550\,P}{N}=\frac{9550\times 15}{1000}=143.25\ \text{N·m}
=1.4325\times 10^{5}\ \text{N·mm}

2) Gear forces on the shaft

Pinion diameter d_g=100\ \text{mm}

F_t=\frac{2T}{d_g}=\frac{2(1.4325\times10^5)}{100}=2865\ \text{N}
F_r=F_t\tan20^\circ=1043\ \text{N}

( F_t horizontal, F_r vertical.)

3) Belt forces at the pulley on the shaft

Pulley D=250\ \text{mm}\Rightarrow v=\frac{\pi DN}{60}
=\frac{\pi\times 0.25\times1000}{60}=13.09\ \text{m/s}

Open belt, take \theta\approx 170^\circ=2.967\ \text{rad} (with motor below).
Tension ratio T_1/T_2=e^{\mu\theta}=e^{0.3\times2.967}=2.435.

Use load-correction 1.2\Rightarrow P_d=1.2P=18\ \text{kW}.

T_1-T_2=\frac{P_d}{v}=\frac{18\,000}{13.09}=1375\ \text{N}
T_2=\frac{1375}{2.435-1}=958\ \text{N},\quad
T_1=2333\ \text{N},\quad
\boxed{F_{\text{belt}}=T_1+T_2\approx 3290\ \text{N (vertical)}}

4) Bearing reactions (two-plane bending)

Take B1 at x=0, B2 at x=300\ \text{mm}; gear at x=120, pulley at x=180.

Vertical plane
Loads: F_r=1043\ \text{N} (down), F_{\text{belt}}=3291\ \text{N} (down)

R_{1v}+R_{2v}=F_r+F_{\text{belt}}=4334
R_{2v}\cdot 300=F_{\text{belt}}\cdot 180 + F_r\cdot 120
\Rightarrow R_{2v}=2392\ \text{N},\ R_{1v}=1942\ \text{N}

Horizontal plane
Only the gear tangential load acts at x=120\ \text{mm}.

R_{1h}+R_{2h}=F_t=2865,\quad R_{2h}\cdot 300 = F_t\cdot 120
\Rightarrow R_{2h}=1146\ \text{N},\ R_{1h}=1719\ \text{N}

Resultant radial loads at bearings
B1:\ R_1=\sqrt{1942^2+1719^2}=2580\ \text{N}
\quad;\quad
B2:\ R_2=\sqrt{2392^2+1146^2}=2650\ \text{N}

5) Maximum bending moment on the shaft

Section of max bending (with the above placement) is near the pulley:

M_v(180)=2.87\times10^5\ \text{N·mm},\quad
M_h(180)=1.38\times10^5\ \text{N·mm}
\boxed{M=\sqrt{M_v^2+M_h^2}\approx 3.18\times10^5\ \text{N·mm}}

6) Shaft diameter (ASME, combined shock)

T=1.4325\times10^5\ \text{N·mm},\quad
K_b=1.5,\ K_t=2.0
T_e=\sqrt{(K_b M)^2+(K_t T)^2}
=\sqrt{(1.5\cdot 3.18\times10^5)^2+(2\cdot 1.4325\times10^5)^2}
=5.57\times10^5\ \text{N·mm}
Allowable shear for ductile shaft with keyway (ASME):
\tau_{\text{allow}}\approx 0.30\,S_{yt}=0.3\times 400=120\ \text{MPa}

d=\left(\frac{16T_e}{\pi \tau_{\text{allow}}}\right)^{1/3}
=\left(\frac{16\times5.57\times10^5}{\pi\times120}\right)^{1/3}
=28.7\ \text{mm}
\ \Rightarrow\ \boxed{d_{\text{use}}=30\ \text{mm}\ (\text{standard})}

7) Key(s) for the gear and pulley (square sunk)

For d=30\ \text{mm}: choose 8×8 mm square key (standard close to d/4).

Check length with torque T (each hub must pass full shaft torque):

l_{\text{shear}}=\frac{2T}{d\,b\,\tau_{\text{allow,key}}}
\quad ;\quad
l_{\text{crush}}=\frac{2T}{d\,t\,\sigma_{c,\text{allow}}}
Take \tau_{\text{allow,key}}\approx 60\ \text{MPa}, \sigma_c\approx 120\ \text{MPa}

l_{\text{shear}}=\frac{2(1.4325\times10^5)}{30\times 8\times 60}=19.9\ \text{mm}
l_{\text{crush}}=9.95\ \text{mm}
\boxed{l\ge 20\ \text{mm};\ \text{use } l=25\text{–}30\ \text{mm (fits 60-mm face)}}

8) Bearings: selection & life

Use deep-groove ball bearings (radial load only; spur gear ⇒ no thrust).

Design (service) load per bearing (given load factor 2.5):
P_{B1}=2.5\,R_1\approx 2.5\times 2.58=6.45\ \text{kN},\quad
P_{B2}\approx 2.5\times 2.65=6.63\ \text{kN}

With a 30-mm shaft, common choices:
	•	6206 (bore 30 mm): C\approx 19\ \text{kN}
	•	6306 (bore 30 mm, heavy series): C\approx 31\ \text{kN}

Basic rating life (balls ⇒ p=3):
L_{10} = \left(\frac{C}{P}\right)^3\!\times 10^6\ \text{rev},\quad
L_h=\frac{L_{10}}{60N}
	•	With 6206: C/P\approx 19/6.5=2.92\Rightarrow L_{10}\approx 25\times10^6\ \text{rev}\Rightarrow L_h\approx \boxed{417\ \text{h}}
	•	With 6306: C/P\approx 31/6.5=4.77\Rightarrow L_{10}\approx 108\times10^6\ \text{rev}\Rightarrow L_h\approx \boxed{1.81\times10^3\ \text{h}}

If your target life is, say, 10,000–20,000 h, you’ll need a much higher C/P ratio (larger series or a different arrangement). With the given “load factor 2.5”, life comes out modest.

9) Belt (quick sizing from given table datum)

Given Dunlop datum: 0.0118\ \text{kW per mm per ply at }5\ \text{m/s}.
At v=13.1\ \text{m/s} the catalog usually applies a speed factor >1; absent a precise factor, a linear speed-scaling is a reasonable first estimate:

\text{capacity per mm per ply} \approx 0.0118\times \frac{13.1}{5}=0.0309\ \text{kW/mm/ply}

Corrected power =1.2\times15=18\ \text{kW}. For a 3-ply belt:
b \approx \frac{18}{3\times 0.0309}\approx \boxed{195\ \text{mm}}
(Use the nearest preferred width above this; refine with the exact Dunlop speed & service factors if your book table gives them.)

10) Gear tooth surface (hardness) – wear (Buckingham) check

For spur gears, limiting wear load is
F_w = d_1\, b_g\, Q\, k
\quad \text{with}\quad Q=\frac{2z_2}{z_1+z_2},\ \
k \propto (\text{BHN})^2
Here z_1=20. Without the mating gear tooth count z_2 explicitly given, a common reduction is i\approx 3 (readers often use the pulley ratio 1.25 for the gear as well, but that wasn’t stated). If you have z_2, plug it in. Set F_w\ge F_t to find the required BHN from your Bhandari/Doyle constant k=K(\text{BHN})^2.
(Because the scan doesn’t show the exact k constant used in your table, I’m leaving this as the direct substitution step. If you tell me the k expression from your data book page, I’ll give the BHN number immediately.)

⸻

What to write in your answer sheet (numbers you can box)
	•	Shaft diameter (ASME): \boxed{30\ \text{mm}}
	•	Key (gear & pulley): \boxed{8\times 8\ \text{mm},\ l\ge 20\ \text{mm (use 25–30 mm)} }
	•	Bearing loads: \boxed{R_1\approx 2.58\ \text{kN},\ R_2\approx 2.65\ \text{kN}}
	•	Suggested bearings: 6206 (≈19 kN) ⇒ L_h\approx 0.42\ \text{kh}; 6306 (≈31 kN) ⇒ L_h\approx 1.8\ \text{kh}.
	•	Belt tensions (at pulley on shaft): \boxed{T_1\approx 2.33\ \text{kN},\ T_2\approx 0.96\ \text{kN},\ T_1+T_2\approx 3.29\ \text{kN}}
	•	Approx. belt width (3-ply, first estimate): \boxed{b\approx 200\ \text{mm}}

If you want me to redo the numbers with your exact distances from the figure (or with the exact Dunlop factors / wear constant k from your data-book table), drop them in and I’ll recalc the final values accordingly.

14/05/26, 13:18 - Sabir Uddin Khan: Dear Sir/Madam,
Good afternoon!
Many department students who have registered for online NPTEL courses in MIS have not entered their NPTEL Roll Number in the MIS portal, despite earlier communication. This is mandatory for the automatic fetching of marks from the Excel sheet downloaded from the NPTEL portal.
Kindly inform the students to complete the entry of their NPTEL Roll Number in MIS if they have registered for online courses.
Also, kindly note that the corresponding NPTEL Subject Code (as mentioned in the NPTEL portal) for the online course offered in MIS should be entered by the Department NPTEL/MIS Coordinator to support the automatic fetching of marks from the Excel sheet.
Please support!
Thank you.
Regards,
UG Section
Academic Office
